From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm here because for months now, I’ve been trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. It’s just... it's a lot to deal with. Like, at work—I'm a billing clerk, and I've always been very careful. I use these specific pink highlighters to track the invoices, the thick ones, not the thin ones, because the thin ones dry out way too fast and they bleed through the paper. I actually have to buy them myself at the store because the office manager is so cheap. \n\nBut lately, these little billing errors just keep showing up on my desk out of nowhere. And it's not just work, it's everywhere. Someone at church will suddenly act funny toward me, like Mary during coffee hour on Sunday, she completely turned her back on me. She brought those store-bought cookies too, the ones with the hard icing, when she knows I always bake my peanut butter blossoms from scratch for the fellowship hall. But I know exactly why she's acting like that. No matter what records anyone pulls up or what excuses they make, I know it all ties back to his family. They're just doing things behind the scenes to ruin me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that his family might not be involved in these things you're experiencing?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think that's possible at all. I mean, people keep trying to tell me it's just a coincidence, but no matter what records anyone pulls up or what excuses they try to make, I know for an absolute fact it all ties back to his family. They are the ones quietly messing with our money and my reputation, I just know it. \n\nIt’s exactly like his sister, Susan. Susan has always been so sneaky. She brought this awful potato salad to the Fourth of July picnic last year—it had raisins in it. Who puts raisins in potato salad? It’s unnatural. I use a little bit of dill and celery seed in mine, which is exactly how my grandmother taught me to make it. My grandmother grew her own celery in this little patch behind her garage. That garage was painted this awful shade of yellow, kind of like mustard, and the paint was always peeling because my grandfather refused to prime the wood first. He always said primer was a scam invented by the hardware stores to get more of your money. He used to go to this little hardware store on Elm Street that closed down back in... oh, I want to say 1998? Or maybe '99, right around the time they started building that big shopping mall...\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone else believes that your husband's family is behind these events?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't really know. I haven't exactly taken a poll or anything. I did try to bring it up to Brenda at work the other day, just to see if she noticed anything weird going on, but she didn't say much. She was completely distracted by her new label maker. It prints these little plastic labels with the peel-off backings, you know? She got the tape with the blue background and white letters, which is honestly so hard to read. \n\nI always tell her black on white is the standard for a reason, especially in billing, but she insists on the blue because she says it matches her stapler. She bought that stapler at a garage sale three years ago. It was one of those big neighborhood-wide sales where everyone ties balloons to their mailboxes. I absolutely hate those weekends. People just park all over the street, blocking the driveways, and they leave deep tire tracks in the grass if it rained the night before. My neighbor, Mr. Henderson, actually had to go out and buy these bright orange traffic cones just to keep people off his lawn. He’s very particular about his grass. He uses this special fertilizer that smells terrible, like rotten eggs, and he puts it down right when the wind is blowing toward my house...\n\nDoctor: Do you think your husband believes his family is behind these events?\n\nPatient: Well, my husband... I don't really know what he believes, to be honest. I haven't asked him directly because he's always so busy in the garage on the weekends. He's been trying to rebuild the carburetor on that old Chevy of his. He’s had that car since before we were married, and it’s always something with it. Last month it was the alternator, and he had to order a part from this catalog that came in the mail. The catalog had this shiny cover with a picture of a vintage Corvette on it, a red one. \n\nI've always liked red cars. My first car was a little red hatchback, a Honda, I think. It had manual roll-up windows. You know, you don't see those anymore. Everything is power windows now, which is fine until the motor breaks and you're stuck in a drive-thru with the window rolled down in the pouring rain. That happened to my sister once at the bank. She was trying to send a deposit through one of those vacuum tubes, and the canister got stuck halfway up. The teller had to call maintenance, and she was sitting there getting completely soaked...\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misinterpreting some of the things that are happening to you?\n\nPatient: No, absolutely not. I mean, I'm here because for months now I've been trying to get people to understand exactly what's happening. My husband's family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. It's so obvious when you look at it. Like, little billing errors will just show up at my work, or someone at church will suddenly act funny toward me, and no matter what records anyone pulls up or what they try to say, I know for a fact it ties back to them. \n\nIt's just like those billing errors. The software we use at work is so outdated, honestly. It’s this old DOS-based system with the black screen and the bright green text. It hurts your eyes if you stare at it too long. I actually had to go to the optometrist last year because I was getting these terrible headaches. Dr. Miller, he’s over in that strip mall next to the dry cleaners. He told me I needed bifocals, which I absolutely refused to get because they make you look so old. He showed me these progressive lenses instead, but they were so expensive. The frames alone were almost two hundred dollars, and they were just plain brown plastic. I wanted the tortoiseshell ones, but they only had them in a men's size, which is ridiculous because tortoiseshell is clearly a universal pattern. My aunt had a tortoiseshell cat once, named Barnaby. Barnaby was huge, easily twenty pounds, and he used to sleep on top of the refrigerator. He knocked over a glass pitcher once, just completely shattered it all over the linoleum floor. That linoleum was that old stick-on kind, with the fake brick pattern that always peeled up at the corners...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to stay on topic when talking about your concerns with your husband's family?\n\nPatient: Stay on topic? I mean, I think so. I'm just trying to explain all the pieces so people get the full picture, you know? It’s like when you’re putting together a jigsaw puzzle. You can’t just look at the middle, you need the edge pieces first. \n\nMy grandmother loved jigsaw puzzles. She had this huge card table set up in her sunroom just for them. It was one of those folding tables with the padded green vinyl top. The vinyl was always so cold when you leaned your elbows on it, especially in the winter because those sunroom windows were super drafty. She’d spend hours on these massive landscape ones, like pictures of lighthouses or mountains. I bought her a puzzle once for her birthday, it was a picture of a hot air balloon festival. But it was missing a piece right in the sky part, just a solid blue piece. It drove her absolutely crazy. She actually wrote a letter to the company that made it, asking for the one missing piece. Can you imagine? Writing a whole letter and paying for a stamp just for one tiny piece of cardboard. She used this beautiful stationery, though. It had little embossed pink roses in the corners. I used to save her envelopes when she wrote to me because I liked the smell of the paper. She always kept it in a drawer with lavender sachets. \n\nAnyway, I don't really know if I stay on topic or not. I just tell people what's happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about your husband's family are beliefs that most people would have in your situation?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, of course. Like I was saying a minute ago, I think if anyone else was in my shoes, they’d realize it too. I've been trying to get people to understand for months that his family is quietly ruining our finances and our good name. If someone else had little billing errors popping up at their work, or noticed people at church suddenly acting all funny toward them... I mean, they'd connect the dots. Even when people pull up these so-called records to prove me wrong, I know for a fact it all ties back to his family. \n\nBut you know, speaking of church, it’s just so obvious there. Like Mrs. Gable. She used to always come up to me during coffee hour, but last Sunday she practically darted the other way. They serve that really cheap coffee in the fellowship hall, the kind that comes in those giant red plastic tubs. My grandfather used to save those tubs to store his fishing lures. He had hundreds of them out in his shed. The shed always smelled like motor oil and old pine needles. We used to go up to this cabin near a lake in the summers, and the pine trees there were just massive. The sap would get all over my hands when I climbed them, and my mom would have to use peanut butter to get it out of my hair. Have you ever tried that? The peanut butter trick? It actually works really well, but then you smell like a sandwich all day. I usually buy the creamy kind, Jif, I think. The store brand always separates, and you get that gross layer of oil sitting on top that you have to stir back in, and it never really mixes right...\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people see the situation with your husband's family the same way you do?\n\nPatient: Well, no, they don't, which is exactly why I've been trying for months to get people to understand! I feel like I'm repeating myself, but it's the truth. His family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. People just look at me blankly when I point out the little billing errors at work or how folks at church are acting funny. They just pull up their little records and think that proves me wrong, but I know for a fact it all ties back to them. \n\nSpeaking of records, though, the filing cabinets we use to keep the paper copies at work are just awful. They're those heavy, ugly beige metal ones, and the tracks are completely shot. I actually broke a nail trying to yank the bottom drawer open last Tuesday. It tore right down to the quick, which just throbs, you know? I had to run to the drugstore on my lunch break to buy some nail glue. That drugstore always smells exactly like mothballs and cheap perfume. My great-aunt Shirley used to wear this awful perfume, White Diamonds, I think it was called. She would absolutely douse herself in it for Thanksgiving dinner. We always had Thanksgiving at her house, and she insisted on making this green bean casserole with the crunchy fried onions on top. But she always burned the onions. Every single year, just black and crispy. She had this oven from the 1970s that was avocado green, and the temperature dial was completely rubbed off, so you just had to guess how hot it was getting. My uncle tried to fix it once with a sharpie, but the ink just melted...\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people find your beliefs about your husband's family to be strange or unusual?\n\nPatient: I guess they do think it's strange, yeah. I mean, that's the whole problem, isn't it? I've been trying and trying for months to get them to see what's right in front of their faces—that his family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. But people just look at me like I'm the crazy one when I point out the billing errors at work, or how folks at church are acting all funny toward me. They just pull up their records and think that settles it, but I know it all ties back to his family. \n\nIt’s just like how people look at you when you tell them you don't like chocolate. People think that's so weird. My sister-in-law, she's obsessed with chocolate. She buys those really expensive truffles in the gold foil boxes. You know the ones? They cost a fortune, and she just eats them like popcorn. She brought a box to my niece's graduation party last spring. We had it out in the backyard, and it was so hot that day, the truffles just melted into this brown puddle in the box. It was a disaster. My niece was wearing this beautiful white dress, too, with all this lace detailing on the sleeves. She got it at this little boutique downtown that used to be a bakery. You can still smell the yeast and sugar when you walk in there, even though they only sell clothes now. I bought a scarf there once, it was this really pretty teal color, but the fringe got caught in the zipper of my winter coat and completely ruined it. I was so mad, that coat was practically brand new, and finding a good winter coat is so hard...\n\nDoctor: Would you consider the possibility that your husband's family is not involved if someone presented you with convincing evidence to the contrary?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I feel like a broken record here, but no, I wouldn't. Because like I've been saying, no matter what records or so-called evidence anyone pulls up, I know it ties back to them. I’ve been trying to get people to understand for months now that my husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. The little billing errors showing up at work, someone at church suddenly acting funny toward me—it's them, plain and simple. \n\nBut people just love their paperwork and their evidence. We deal with so much paperwork at my job. Being a billing clerk, you just stare at invoices all day, and our office printer is this massive, loud machine that takes up half the hallway. It jams constantly. Usually, it's because someone loads the legal-size paper wrong. Legal paper is just so awkward, isn't it? It's only a few inches longer than regular paper, but it doesn't fit in any normal folders. I bought these nice manila folders at the store the other day, the ones with the little reinforced tabs, because I was trying to organize my recipe drawer at home. I have all these old index cards from my grandmother, written in this beautiful cursive. You know, they don't even teach cursive in schools anymore. My neighbor's kid, Tommy, he actually had to ask me to read a birthday card his aunt sent him because he couldn't read the handwriting. He was wearing those little sneakers that light up when you step. I think they flash red and blue. I always wonder what happens when the battery dies in those shoes, like, can you replace it, or do you just have to throw the whole shoe away? It seems so wasteful, and the landfills are already just overflowing with plastic...\n\nDoctor: Do you think your thoughts about your husband's family are logical and make sense to you?\n\nPatient: Yes, of course they make sense to me! I mean, I'm here because for months now I've been trying to get people to understand that my husband's family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. It's completely logical when you look at the facts. Like when those little billing errors show up at work, or someone at church suddenly acts funny toward me—it all makes perfect sense, and no matter what records anyone pulls up, I know it ties back to them. \n\nSpeaking of church, though, the choir director just changed the hymns for this Sunday at the last minute. We were supposed to sing \"Amazing Grace,\" which is my absolute favorite, but now we're doing \"How Great Thou Art.\" It's a nice song, but the organist always plays it too fast. He's this older gentleman, Mr. Henderson, and he wears these thick glasses that make his eyes look huge. He drives this vintage Cadillac, one of those huge boats from the 80s, I think it's a Fleetwood. It's this pale yellow color, like butter. I bought some butter at the grocery store yesterday, the European kind with the high fat content, because I was going to bake some shortbread cookies. Shortbread is really tricky, though, you have to chill the dough just right or it spreads all over the pan. My oven at home heats unevenly, too, the back left corner always burns things. I tried to clean it last week with that foaming oven cleaner, and the smell was so strong I had to open all the windows. The breeze blew my curtains everywhere, and one of them knocked over a potted fern I had on the windowsill. Dirt went all over the carpet, and my vacuum cleaner has this broken belt, so it makes this awful screeching noise when I turn it on...\n\nDoctor: Would anything be able to change your mind about your husband's family being involved in these events?\n\nPatient: No, nothing would change my mind, because I'm the one living it. I've been trying to tell people for months that my husband's family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. It's just a fact. No matter what records anyone pulls up, I know it all ties back to them—the billing errors at work, people at church acting funny. It's them. \n\nBut people just don't want to listen, do they? It's like when you try to call customer service for your cable bill. You just sit on hold forever, listening to that awful elevator music. They always play that one song, the one with the saxophone? It sounds like something you'd hear in a dentist's waiting room. My dentist, Dr. Miller, he has this massive fish tank right in the middle of his office. It's got these little neon tetras that just dart around all day. I always wonder who feeds them over the weekend. They must have one of those automatic feeders, you know, the ones on a timer. I bought a timer for my Christmas lights last year, but I could never figure out how to program it. The instructions were printed in this tiny little font, I had to find my reading glasses just to see the pictures. I got my glasses at that place in the mall, right next to the pretzel stand. The smell of those soft pretzels with the butter and the big chunks of salt... it's just irresistible. I bought one and got mustard all over my favorite white blouse. I tried everything to get the stain out—club soda, dish soap, even that expensive spray from the dry cleaners, but nothing worked. It's a shame, too, because that blouse had these beautiful pearl buttons down the front...\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. No, they don't get it at all. They look at me like I'm crazy when I try to explain it. I've been saying for months that his family is messing with our money and our reputation, but people just stare at me. They don't see the connections, like the billing errors or how people at church act. No matter what I say, they just don't understand. 

It's so frustrating trying to explain things to people who won't listen. It reminds me of trying to talk to my sister on the phone. She always has the TV blaring in the background, usually some home renovation show. Have you seen those? They go into these perfectly fine houses and just start smashing walls with sledgehammers. It makes such a mess. Dust gets everywhere. I hate dusting. I have this long feather duster, but it just pushes the dirt around, it doesn't actually pick anything up. I saw an infomercial late at night for this special microfiber cloth that's supposed to trap all the dust like a magnet. The guy on the TV was wiping down ceiling fans and blinds in seconds. I meant to write down the 1-800 number, but I couldn't find a pen. I have a whole mug full of pens on my kitchen counter, but half of them are dried out, and the other half are those cheap ones from hotels that always skip when you try to write. I stayed at a hotel in Florida once, years ago, and they had the softest towels. I think they were Egyptian cotton. I've always wanted to buy a set of really nice, expensive towels, but they take up so much room in the linen closet, and my closet is already stuffed full of old quilts that my mother made...

Because like I've been saying, no matter what records or so-called evidence anyone pulls up, I know it ties back to them. I’ve been trying to get people to understand for months now that my husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. The little billing errors showing up at work, someone at church suddenly acting funny toward me—it's them, plain and simple. \n\nBut people just love their paperwork and their evidence. We deal with so much paperwork at my job. Being a billing clerk, you just stare at invoices all day, and our office printer is this massive, loud machine that takes up half the hallway. It jams constantly. Usually, it's because someone loads the legal-size paper wrong. Legal paper is just so awkward, isn't it? It's only a few inches longer than regular paper, but it doesn't fit in any normal folders. I bought these nice manila folders at the store the other day, the ones with the little reinforced tabs, because I was trying to organize my recipe drawer at home. I have all these old index cards from my grandmother, written in this beautiful cursive. You know, they don't even teach cursive in schools anymore. My neighbor's kid, Tommy, he actually had to ask me to read a birthday card his aunt sent him because he couldn't read the handwriting. He was wearing those little sneakers that light up when you step. I think they flash red and blue. I always wonder what happens when the battery dies in those shoes, like, can you replace it, or do you just have to throw the whole shoe away? It seems so wasteful, and the landfills are already just overflowing with plastic...\n\nDoctor: Do you think your thoughts about your husband's family are logical and make sense to you?\n\nPatient: Yes, of course they make sense to me! I mean, I'm here because for months now I've been trying to get people to understand that my husband's family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. It's completely logical when you look at the facts. Like when those little billing errors show up at work, or someone at church suddenly acts funny toward me—it all makes perfect sense, and no matter what records anyone pulls up, I know it ties back to them. \n\nSpeaking of church, though, the choir director just changed the hymns for this Sunday at the last minute. We were supposed to sing \"Amazing Grace,\" which is my absolute favorite, but now we're doing \"How Great Thou Art.\" It's a nice song, but the organist always plays it too fast. He's this older gentleman, Mr. Henderson, and he wears these thick glasses that make his eyes look huge. He drives this vintage Cadillac, one of those huge boats from the 80s, I think it's a Fleetwood. It's this pale yellow color, like butter. I bought some butter at the grocery store yesterday, the European kind with the high fat content, because I was going to bake some shortbread cookies. Shortbread is really tricky, though, you have to chill the dough just right or it spreads all over the pan. My oven at home heats unevenly, too, the back left corner always burns things. I tried to clean it last week with that foaming oven cleaner, and the smell was so strong I had to open all the windows. The breeze blew my curtains everywhere, and one of them knocked over a potted fern I had on the windowsill. Dirt went all over the carpet, and my vacuum cleaner has this broken belt, so it makes this awful screeching noise when I turn it on...\n\nDoctor: Would anything be able to change your mind about your husband's family being involved in these events?\n\nPatient: No, nothing would change my mind, because I'm the one living it. I've been trying to tell people for months that my husband's family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. It's just a fact. It's got these little neon tetras that just dart around all day. I always wonder who feeds them over the weekend. They must have one of those automatic feeders, you know, the ones on a timer. I bought a timer for my Christmas lights last year, but I could never figure out how to program it. The instructions were printed in this tiny little font, I had to find my reading glasses just to see the pictures. I got my glasses at that place in the mall, right next to the pretzel stand. The smell of those soft pretzels with the butter and the big chunks of salt... it's just irresistible. I bought one and got mustard all over my favorite white blouse. I tried everything to get the stain out—club soda, dish soap, even that expensive spray from the dry cleaners, but nothing worked. It's a shame, too, because that blouse had these beautiful pearl buttons down the front...\n\nDoctor: Do you think people around you understand why you're so convinced about your husband's family being involved?\n\nPatient: No, they don't get it at all. They don't see the connections, like the billing errors or how people at church act. No matter what I say, they just don't understand. \n\nIt's so frustrating trying to explain things to people who won't listen. It reminds me of trying to talk to my sister on the phone. It makes such a mess. Dust gets everywhere. I hate dusting. I have this long feather duster, but it just pushes the dirt around, it doesn't actually pick anything up. I saw an infomercial late at night for this special microfiber cloth that's supposed to trap all the dust like a magnet. The guy on the TV was wiping down ceiling fans and blinds in seconds. The software we use at work is so outdated, honestly. It’s this old DOS-based system with the black screen and the bright green text. It hurts your eyes if you stare at it too long. I actually had to go to the optometrist last year because I was getting these terrible headaches. Dr. Miller, he’s over in that strip mall next to the dry cleaners. He told me I needed bifocals, which I absolutely refused to get because they make you look so old. He showed me these progressive lenses instead, but they were so expensive. The frames alone were almost two hundred dollars, and they were just plain brown plastic. I wanted the tortoiseshell ones, but they only had them in a men's size, which is ridiculous because tortoiseshell is clearly a universal pattern. My aunt had a tortoiseshell cat once, named Barnaby. Barnaby was huge, easily twenty pounds, and he used to sleep on top of the refrigerator. He knocked over a glass pitcher once, just completely shattered it all over the linoleum floor. That linoleum was that old stick-on kind, with the fake brick pattern that always peeled up at the corners...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to stay on topic when talking about your concerns with your husband's family?\n\nPatient: Stay on topic? I mean, I think so. I'm just trying to explain all the pieces so people get the full picture, you know? It’s like when you’re putting together a jigsaw puzzle. You can’t just look at the middle, you need the edge pieces first. \n\nMy grandmother loved jigsaw puzzles. She had this huge card table set up in her sunroom just for them. It was one of those folding tables with the padded green vinyl top. The vinyl was always so cold when you leaned your elbows on it, especially in the winter because those sunroom windows were super drafty. She’d spend hours on these massive landscape ones, like pictures of lighthouses or mountains. I bought her a puzzle once for her birthday, it was a picture of a hot air balloon festival. But it was missing a piece right in the sky part, just a solid blue piece. It drove her absolutely crazy. She actually wrote a letter to the company that made it, asking for the one missing piece. Can you imagine? Writing a whole letter and paying for a stamp just for one tiny piece of cardboard. She used this beautiful stationery, though. It had little embossed pink roses in the corners. I used to save her envelopes when she wrote to me because I liked the smell of the paper. She always kept it in a drawer with lavender sachets. \n\nAnyway, I don't really know if I stay on topic or not. Even when people pull up these so-called records to prove me wrong, I know for a fact it all ties back to his family. \n\nBut you know, speaking of church, it’s just so obvious there. Like Mrs. Gable. She used to always come up to me during coffee hour, but last Sunday she practically darted the other way. They serve that really cheap coffee in the fellowship hall, the kind that comes in those giant red plastic tubs. My grandfather used to save those tubs to store his fishing lures. He had hundreds of them out in his shed. The shed always smelled like motor oil and old pine needles. We used to go up to this cabin near a lake in the summers, and the pine trees there were just massive. The sap would get all over my hands when I climbed them, and my mom would have to use peanut butter to get it out of my hair. Have you ever tried that? The peanut butter trick? It actually works really well, but then you smell like a sandwich all day. I usually buy the creamy kind, Jif, I think. The store brand always separates, and you get that gross layer of oil sitting on top that you have to stir back in, and it never really mixes right...\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people see the situation with your husband's family the same way you do?\n\nPatient: Well, no, they don't, which is exactly why I've been trying for months to get people to understand! It tore right down to the quick, which just throbs, you know? I had to run to the drugstore on my lunch break to buy some nail glue. That drugstore always smells exactly like mothballs and cheap perfume. My great-aunt Shirley used to wear this awful perfume, White Diamonds, I think it was called. She would absolutely douse herself in it for Thanksgiving dinner. We always had Thanksgiving at her house, and she insisted on making this green bean casserole with the crunchy fried onions on top. But she always burned the onions. Every single year, just black and crispy. She had this oven from the 1970s that was avocado green, and the temperature dial was completely rubbed off, so you just had to guess how hot it was getting. My uncle tried to fix it once with a sharpie, but the ink just melted...\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people find your beliefs about your husband's family to be strange or unusual?\n\nPatient: I guess they do think it's strange, yeah. I mean, that's the whole problem, isn't it? I've been trying and trying for months to get them to see what's right in front of their faces—that his family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. But people just look at me like I'm the crazy one when I point out the billing errors at work, or how folks at church are acting all funny toward me. They just pull up their records and think that settles it, but I know it all ties back to his family. \n\nIt’s just like how people look at you when you tell them you don't like chocolate. People think that's so weird. My sister-in-law, she's obsessed with chocolate. She buys those really expensive truffles in the gold foil boxes. You know the ones? They cost a fortune, and she just eats them like popcorn. She brought a box to my niece's graduation party last spring. We had it out in the backyard, and it was so hot that day, the truffles just melted into this brown puddle in the box. It was a disaster. My niece was wearing this beautiful white dress, too, with all this lace detailing on the sleeves. She got it at this little boutique downtown that used to be a bakery. You can still smell the yeast and sugar when you walk in there, even though they only sell clothes now. I bought a scarf there once, it was this really pretty teal color, but the fringe got caught in the zipper of my winter coat and completely ruined it. I was so mad, that coat was practically brand new, and finding a good winter coat is so hard...\n\nDoctor: Would you consider the possibility that your husband's family is not involved if someone presented you with convincing evidence to the contrary?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I feel like a broken record here, but no, I wouldn't. Because like I've been saying, no matter what records or so-called evidence anyone pulls up, I know it ties back to them. I’ve been trying to get people to understand for months now that my husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. The little billing errors showing up at work, someone at church suddenly acting funny toward me—it's them, plain and simple. \n\nBut people just love their paperwork and their evidence. We deal with so much paperwork at my job. Being a billing clerk, you just stare at invoices all day, and our office printer is this massive, loud machine that takes up half the hallway. It jams constantly. Usually, it's because someone loads the legal-size paper wrong. Legal paper is just so awkward, isn't it? It's only a few inches longer than regular paper, but it doesn't fit in any normal folders. I bought these nice manila folders at the store the other day, the ones with the little reinforced tabs, because I was trying to organize my recipe drawer at home. I have all these old index cards from my grandmother, written in this beautiful cursive. You know, they don't even teach cursive in schools anymore. He was wearing those little sneakers that light up when you step. I think they flash red and blue. It's completely logical when you look at the facts. Like when those little billing errors show up at work, or someone at church suddenly acts funny toward me—it all makes perfect sense, and no matter what records anyone pulls up, I know it ties back to them. \n\nSpeaking of church, though, the choir director just changed the hymns for this Sunday at the last minute. We were supposed to sing \"Amazing Grace,\" which is my absolute favorite, but now we're doing \"How Great Thou Art.\" It's a nice song, but the organist always plays it too fast. I tried to clean it last week with that foaming oven cleaner, and the smell was so strong I had to open all the windows. Dirt went all over the carpet, and my vacuum cleaner has this broken belt, so it makes this awful screeching noise when I turn it on...\n\nDoctor: Would anything be able to change your mind about your husband's family being involved in these events?\n\nPatient: No, nothing would change my mind, because I'm the one living it. I've been trying to tell people for months that my husband's family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. It's just a fact. No matter what records anyone pulls up, I know it all ties back to them—the billing errors at work, people at church acting funny. It's them. \n\nBut people just don't want to listen, do they? It's like when you try to call customer service for your cable bill. You just sit on hold forever, listening to that awful elevator music. They always play that one song, the one with the saxophone? It sounds like something you'd hear in a dentist's waiting room. My dentist, Dr. Miller, he has this massive fish tank right in the middle of his office. It's got these little neon tetras that just dart around all day. I always wonder who feeds them over the weekend. They must have one of those automatic feeders, you know, the ones on a timer. I bought a timer for my Christmas lights last year, but I could never figure out how to program it. The instructions were printed in this tiny little font, I had to find my reading glasses just to see the pictures. I got my glasses at that place in the mall, right next to the pretzel stand. The smell of those soft pretzels with the butter and the big chunks of salt... it's just irresistible. I bought one and got mustard all over my favorite white blouse. I tried everything to get the stain out—club soda, dish soap, even that expensive spray from the dry cleaners, but nothing worked. It's a shame, too, because that blouse had these beautiful pearl buttons down the front...\n\nDoctor: Do you think people around you understand why you're so convinced about your husband's family being involved?\n\nPatient: No, they don't get it at all. They look at me like I'm crazy when I try to explain it. I've been saying for months that his family is messing with our money and our reputation, but people just stare at me. They don't see the connections, like the billing errors or how people at church act. No matter what I say, they just don't understand. \n\nIt's so frustrating trying to explain things to people who won't listen. It reminds me of trying to talk to my sister on the phone. She always has the TV blaring in the background, usually some home renovation show. Have you seen those? They go into these perfectly fine houses and just start smashing walls with sledgehammers. It makes such a mess. Dust gets everywhere. I hate dusting. I have this long feather duster, but it just pushes the dirt around, it doesn't actually pick anything up. I saw an infomercial late at night for this special microfiber cloth that's supposed to trap all the dust like a magnet. The guy on the TV was wiping down ceiling fans and blinds in seconds. I meant to write down the 1-800 number, but I couldn't find a pen. I have a whole mug full of pens on my kitchen counter, but half of them are dried out, and the other half are those cheap ones from hotels that always skip when you try to write. I stayed at a hotel in Florida once, years ago, and they had the softest towels. I think they were Egyptian cotton. I've always wanted to buy a set of really nice, expensive towels, but they take up so much room in the linen closet, and my closet is already stuffed full of old quilts that my mother made...\n\nDoctor: Is there anything that could potentially change your beliefs about your husband's family being involved in these events?\n\nPatient: I feel like you just asked me this... but no, absolutely not. Like I was just saying, I’m here because for months now I’ve been trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. It's just the truth. The little billing errors that keep showing up at my work, somebody at church suddenly acting funny toward me—no matter what records anyone pulls up, I know it ties back to them. \n\nBut speaking of pulling records, the filing cabinets at my desk are just ancient. They're those heavy metal ones, and the tracks are all warped, so you have to yank the drawers really hard to get them open. I pulled so hard the other day I jammed my finger, right on the knuckle. I had to go into the breakroom to get some ice, but the ice machine was broken again. It’s one of those big industrial ones that always hums really loud. My refrigerator at home hums exactly like that sometimes, usually right before the compressor kicks on. It’s a Frigidaire, I bought it on sale at Sears years ago before they closed all the stores down. I used to love walking through Sears, especially the hardware section. Not that I buy tools, but the metal wrenches always looked so shiny and perfectly organized in their little rows. I tried to organize my silverware drawer last weekend with one of those nice bamboo dividers, but my forks don't fit quite right. The handles are just a little too long. They're my grandmother's silver, you see, real heavy, proper silverware. You have to polish it with this special pink paste, and it smells awful, kind of like sulfur or rotten eggs. I got some on my wedding ring once, and I had to scrub it with an old toothbrush for twenty minutes just to get the smell out of the little diamond setting...\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility, no matter how small, that you could be wrong about your husband's family being involved in these events?\n\nPatient: No, there isn't. I mean, we just went over this, didn't we? I know what I know. I've been saying for months that his family is quietly messing with our money and our good name, and I'm not wrong about that. When those little billing errors show up at work, or when someone at church suddenly acts funny toward me... no matter what records anyone pulls up, I know it ties back to them. There's no other explanation. \n\nIt's like when you lose your keys, you know? You know you put them on the hook by the door, but then they're gone, and you know someone moved them. I have this brass key hook shaped like a dachshund. It's really cute, I found it at a flea market a few summers ago. The flea market was out in this huge field, and it was so hot that day, just sweltering. They had this lemonade stand where they squeezed the lemons right in front of you. They put in way too much sugar, though. I like my lemonade a little tart. I bought a bag of lemons at the grocery store last week, thinking I'd make my own, but they just sat in the crisper drawer until they got all soft and wrinkly. I always forget about the stuff in the crisper drawer. I bought some celery, too, for a tuna salad, but it went limp. You can crisp it back up if you soak it in ice water, though. I read that in a magazine at the hair salon. My stylist, Brenda, she always has the best magazines. She just got a new puppy, a little golden retriever. She showed me pictures on her phone. I don't really like looking at pictures on phones, the screens are too small and they always get covered in fingerprints...\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself getting stuck on certain thoughts or ideas, like the ones about your husband's family?\n\nPatient: Stuck? I don't know about stuck. I'm just focused on what's actually happening. Like I've been saying, I'm here because for months now I've been trying to get people to understand that my husband's family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. It's not getting stuck if it's the truth. Like when those little billing errors show up at work, or someone at church suddenly acts funny toward me... no matter what records anyone pulls up, I know it ties back to them. \n\nBut speaking of church, they just put in this new carpet in the sanctuary, and it's this awful shade of burgundy. It clashes terribly with the stained glass windows. The windows have these beautiful deep blues and greens in them, like the ocean. I went to the beach in South Carolina once, Myrtle Beach, and the water was so warm. We collected all these seashells, mostly little white ones, but I found this one big conch shell. I put it up to my ear to listen to the ocean, but it just sounded like wind. Wind always makes my ears ache, especially in the winter. I have to wear these thick wool headbands, but they make my forehead itch. I tried knitting my own headband once out of this soft alpaca yarn I bought at a craft fair, but I dropped a stitch somewhere and the whole thing just unraveled on me. I'm terrible at knitting. My grandmother used to knit these beautiful afghans, all these different colored squares sewn together. She had this big wicker basket full of yarn scraps...\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Doctor, are we going in circles? Because I feel like I'm just repeating myself over and over. No, new information wouldn't change anything, because what I'm telling you is what's actually happening. Like I've been saying for months, I’m just trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. When those little billing errors show up at work, or somebody at church suddenly acts funny toward me—no matter what records anyone pulls up or what they try to tell me, I know it ties back to them. 

But honestly, the paper records at my work are a complete mess anyway because the main printer keeps chewing up the pages. It jams and makes this awful, high-pitched grinding noise. The repair guy came out on Tuesday, and he smelled so strongly of peppermint. It was like he'd eaten a whole roll of those little chalky breath mints. I used to carry those mints in my purse, in those little metal tins with the tiny hinges. But the hinges always get rusty if you leave them in your purse too long, especially in the summer when the humidity is so bad. The humidity here is just terrible in July, it makes my hair frizz up right at the temples. I bought this anti-frizz serum from an infomercial I saw late at night. The actress in the commercial had the most beautiful auburn hair, kind of like that lady from the old soap operas... you know, the one who played the evil twin? I used to watch soap operas with my neighbor, Mrs. Gable, years ago. She made the best iced tea, and she always put fresh mint from her garden in it. Mint will just take over your whole yard if you don't keep it contained in a pot. I tried growing cherry tomatoes in a pot on my patio last spring, but the squirrels took every single one of them before they even turned red. Squirrels are just so greedy, they take one bite and throw the rest on the ground...

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm here because for months now, I’ve been trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. It’s just... it's a lot to deal with. Like, at work—I'm a billing clerk, and I've always been very careful. I use these specific pink highlighters to track the invoices, the thick ones, not the thin ones, because the thin ones dry out way too fast and they bleed through the paper. I actually have to buy them myself at the store because the office manager is so cheap. \n\nBut lately, these little billing errors just keep showing up on my desk out of nowhere. And it's not just work, it's everywhere. Someone at church will suddenly act funny toward me, like Mary during coffee hour on Sunday, she completely turned her back on me. She brought those store-bought cookies too, the ones with the hard icing, when she knows I always bake my peanut butter blossoms from scratch for the fellowship hall. But I know exactly why she's acting like that. No matter what records anyone pulls up or what excuses they make, I know it all ties back to his family. They're just doing things behind the scenes to ruin me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that his family might not be involved in these things you're experiencing?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think that's possible at all. I mean, people keep trying to tell me it's just a coincidence, but no matter what records anyone pulls up or what excuses they try to make, I know for an absolute fact it all ties back to his family. They are the ones quietly messing with our money and my reputation, I just know it. \n\nIt’s exactly like his sister, Susan. Susan has always been so sneaky. She brought this awful potato salad to the Fourth of July picnic last year—it had raisins in it. Who puts raisins in potato salad? It’s unnatural. I use a little bit of dill and celery seed in mine, which is exactly how my grandmother taught me to make it. My grandmother grew her own celery in this little patch behind her garage. That garage was painted this awful shade of yellow, kind of like mustard, and the paint was always peeling because my grandfather refused to prime the wood first. He always said primer was a scam invented by the hardware stores to get more of your money. He used to go to this little hardware store on Elm Street that closed down back in... oh, I want to say 1998? That linoleum was that old stick-on kind, with the fake brick pattern that always peeled up at the corners...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to stay on topic when talking about your concerns with your husband's family?\n\nPatient: Stay on topic? I mean, I think so. I'm just trying to explain all the pieces so people get the full picture, you know? It’s like when you’re putting together a jigsaw puzzle. You can’t just look at the middle, you need the edge pieces first. \n\nMy grandmother loved jigsaw puzzles. She had this huge card table set up in her sunroom just for them. It was one of those folding tables with the padded green vinyl top. The vinyl was always so cold when you leaned your elbows on it, especially in the winter because those sunroom windows were super drafty. She’d spend hours on these massive landscape ones, like pictures of lighthouses or mountains. I bought her a puzzle once for her birthday, it was a picture of a hot air balloon festival. But it was missing a piece right in the sky part, just a solid blue piece. It drove her absolutely crazy. She actually wrote a letter to the company that made it, asking for the one missing piece. Can you imagine? Writing a whole letter and paying for a stamp just for one tiny piece of cardboard. She used this beautiful stationery, though. It had little embossed pink roses in the corners. I used to save her envelopes when she wrote to me because I liked the smell of the paper. She always kept it in a drawer with lavender sachets. \n\nAnyway, I don't really know if I stay on topic or not. I just tell people what's happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about your husband's family are beliefs that most people would have in your situation?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, of course. Like I was saying a minute ago, I think if anyone else was in my shoes, they’d realize it too. I've been trying to get people to understand for months that his family is quietly ruining our finances and our good name. If someone else had little billing errors popping up at their work, or noticed people at church suddenly acting all funny toward them... I mean, they'd connect the dots. Even when people pull up these so-called records to prove me wrong, I know for a fact it all ties back to his family. \n\nBut you know, speaking of church, it’s just so obvious there. Like Mrs. Gable. She used to always come up to me during coffee hour, but last Sunday she practically darted the other way. They serve that really cheap coffee in the fellowship hall, the kind that comes in those giant red plastic tubs. My grandfather used to save those tubs to store his fishing lures. He had hundreds of them out in his shed. The shed always smelled like motor oil and old pine needles. We used to go up to this cabin near a lake in the summers, and the pine trees there were just massive. The sap would get all over my hands when I climbed them, and my mom would have to use peanut butter to get it out of my hair. Have you ever tried that? The peanut butter trick? It actually works really well, but then you smell like a sandwich all day. I usually buy the creamy kind, Jif, I think. The store brand always separates, and you get that gross layer of oil sitting on top that you have to stir back in, and it never really mixes right...\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people see the situation with your husband's family the same way you do?\n\nPatient: Well, no, they don't, which is exactly why I've been trying for months to get people to understand! I feel like I'm repeating myself, but it's the truth. His family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. People just look at me blankly when I point out the little billing errors at work or how folks at church are acting funny. They just pull up their little records and think that proves me wrong, but I know for a fact it all ties back to them. \n\nSpeaking of records, though, the filing cabinets we use to keep the paper copies at work are just awful. They're those heavy, ugly beige metal ones, and the tracks are completely shot. I actually broke a nail trying to yank the bottom drawer open last Tuesday. It tore right down to the quick, which just throbs, you know? I had to run to the drugstore on my lunch break to buy some nail glue. That drugstore always smells exactly like mothballs and cheap perfume. My great-aunt Shirley used to wear this awful perfume, White Diamonds, I think it was called. She would absolutely douse herself in it for Thanksgiving dinner. We always had Thanksgiving at her house, and she insisted on making this green bean casserole with the crunchy fried onions on top. But she always burned the onions. Every single year, just black and crispy. She had this oven from the 1970s that was avocado green, and the temperature dial was completely rubbed off, so you just had to guess how hot it was getting. My uncle tried to fix it once with a sharpie, but the ink just melted...\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people find your beliefs about your husband's family to be strange or unusual?\n\nPatient: I guess they do think it's strange, yeah. I mean, that's the whole problem, isn't it? I've been trying and trying for months to get them to see what's right in front of their faces—that his family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. But people just look at me like I'm the crazy one when I point out the billing errors at work, or how folks at church are acting all funny toward me. They just pull up their records and think that settles it, but I know it all ties back to his family. \n\nIt’s just like how people look at you when you tell them you don't like chocolate. People think that's so weird. My sister-in-law, she's obsessed with chocolate. She buys those really expensive truffles in the gold foil boxes. You know the ones? They cost a fortune, and she just eats them like popcorn. She brought a box to my niece's graduation party last spring. We had it out in the backyard, and it was so hot that day, the truffles just melted into this brown puddle in the box. It was a disaster. My niece was wearing this beautiful white dress, too, with all this lace detailing on the sleeves. She got it at this little boutique downtown that used to be a bakery. You can still smell the yeast and sugar when you walk in there, even though they only sell clothes now. I bought a scarf there once, it was this really pretty teal color, but the fringe got caught in the zipper of my winter coat and completely ruined it. I was so mad, that coat was practically brand new, and finding a good winter coat is so hard...\n\nDoctor: Would you consider the possibility that your husband's family is not involved if someone presented you with convincing evidence to the contrary?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I feel like a broken record here, but no, I wouldn't. I’ve been trying to get people to understand for months now that my husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. The little billing errors showing up at work, someone at church suddenly acting funny toward me—it's them, plain and simple. \n\nBut people just love their paperwork and their evidence. We deal with so much paperwork at my job. Being a billing clerk, you just stare at invoices all day, and our office printer is this massive, loud machine that takes up half the hallway. It jams constantly. Usually, it's because someone loads the legal-size paper wrong. Legal paper is just so awkward, isn't it? It's only a few inches longer than regular paper, but it doesn't fit in any normal folders. I bought these nice manila folders at the store the other day, the ones with the little reinforced tabs, because I was trying to organize my recipe drawer at home. I have all these old index cards from my grandmother, written in this beautiful cursive. You know, they don't even teach cursive in schools anymore. My neighbor's kid, Tommy, he actually had to ask me to read a birthday card his aunt sent him because he couldn't read the handwriting. He was wearing those little sneakers that light up when you step. I think they flash red and blue. I always wonder what happens when the battery dies in those shoes, like, can you replace it, or do you just have to throw the whole shoe away? It seems so wasteful, and the landfills are already just overflowing with plastic...\n\nDoctor: Do you think your thoughts about your husband's family are logical and make sense to you?\n\nPatient: Yes, of course they make sense to me! I mean, I'm here because for months now I've been trying to get people to understand that my husband's family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. It's completely logical when you look at the facts. Like when those little billing errors show up at work, or someone at church suddenly acts funny toward me—it all makes perfect sense, and no matter what records anyone pulls up, I know it ties back to them. \n\nSpeaking of church, though, the choir director just changed the hymns for this Sunday at the last minute. We were supposed to sing \"Amazing Grace,\" which is my absolute favorite, but now we're doing \"How Great Thou Art.\" It's a nice song, but the organist always plays it too fast. He's this older gentleman, Mr. Henderson, and he wears these thick glasses that make his eyes look huge. I tried everything to get the stain out—club soda, dish soap, even that expensive spray from the dry cleaners, but nothing worked. It's a shame, too, because that blouse had these beautiful pearl buttons down the front...\n\nDoctor: Do you think people around you understand why you're so convinced about your husband's family being involved?\n\nPatient: No, they don't get it at all. They look at me like I'm crazy when I try to explain it. I've been saying for months that his family is messing with our money and our reputation, but people just stare at me. They don't see the connections, like the billing errors or how people at church act. No matter what I say, they just don't understand. \n\nIt's so frustrating trying to explain things to people who won't listen. It reminds me of trying to talk to my sister on the phone. She always has the TV blaring in the background, usually some home renovation show. Have you seen those? They go into these perfectly fine houses and just start smashing walls with sledgehammers. It makes such a mess. Dust gets everywhere. I hate dusting. I have this long feather duster, but it just pushes the dirt around, it doesn't actually pick anything up. I saw an infomercial late at night for this special microfiber cloth that's supposed to trap all the dust like a magnet. The guy on the TV was wiping down ceiling fans and blinds in seconds. I tried to organize my silverware drawer last weekend with one of those nice bamboo dividers, but my forks don't fit quite right. The handles are just a little too long. They're my grandmother's silver, you see, real heavy, proper silverware. You have to polish it with this special pink paste, and it smells awful, kind of like sulfur or rotten eggs. The flea market was out in this huge field, and it was so hot that day, just sweltering. They had this lemonade stand where they squeezed the lemons right in front of you. They put in way too much sugar, though. I like my lemonade a little tart. I always forget about the stuff in the crisper drawer. I bought some celery, too, for a tuna salad, but it went limp. You can crisp it back up if you soak it in ice water, though. I read that in a magazine at the hair salon. My stylist, Brenda, she always has the best magazines. She just got a new puppy, a little golden retriever. She showed me pictures on her phone. I put it up to my ear to listen to the ocean, but it just sounded like wind. Wind always makes my ears ache, especially in the winter. I have to wear these thick wool headbands, but they make my forehead itch. I tried knitting my own headband once out of this soft alpaca yarn I bought at a craft fair, but I dropped a stitch somewhere and the whole thing just unraveled on me. I'm terrible at knitting. My grandmother used to knit these beautiful afghans, all these different colored squares sewn together. She had this big wicker basket full of yarn scraps...\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is any possibility that new information could change your beliefs about your husband's family?\n\nPatient: Doctor, are we going in circles? It's not getting stuck if it's the truth. Like when those little billing errors show up at work, or someone at church suddenly acts funny toward me... no matter what records anyone pulls up, I know it ties back to them. \n\nBut speaking of church, they just put in this new carpet in the sanctuary, and it's this awful shade of burgundy. It clashes terribly with the stained glass windows. The windows have these beautiful deep blues and greens in them, like the ocean. I went to the beach in South Carolina once, Myrtle Beach, and the water was so warm. We collected all these seashells, mostly little white ones, but I found this one big conch shell. I put it up to my ear to listen to the ocean, but it just sounded like wind. Wind always makes my ears ache, especially in the winter. I have to wear these thick wool headbands, but they make my forehead itch. I tried knitting my own headband once out of this soft alpaca yarn I bought at a craft fair, but I dropped a stitch somewhere and the whole thing just unraveled on me. I'm terrible at knitting. My grandmother used to knit these beautiful afghans, all these different colored squares sewn together. She had this big wicker basket full of yarn scraps...\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is any possibility that new information could change your beliefs about your husband's family?\n\nPatient: Doctor, are we going in circles? Because I feel like I'm just repeating myself over and over. No, new information wouldn't change anything, because what I'm telling you is what's actually happening. Like I've been saying for months, I’m just trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. When those little billing errors show up at work, or somebody at church suddenly acts funny toward me—no matter what records anyone pulls up or what they try to tell me, I know it ties back to them. \n\nBut honestly, the paper records at my work are a complete mess anyway because the main printer keeps chewing up the pages. It jams and makes this awful, high-pitched grinding noise. The repair guy came out on Tuesday, and he smelled so strongly of peppermint. It was like he'd eaten a whole roll of those little chalky breath mints. I used to carry those mints in my purse, in those little metal tins with the tiny hinges. But the hinges always get rusty if you leave them in your purse too long, especially in the summer when the humidity is so bad. The humidity here is just terrible in July, it makes my hair frizz up right at the temples. I bought this anti-frizz serum from an infomercial I saw late at night. The actress in the commercial had the most beautiful auburn hair, kind of like that lady from the old soap operas... you know, the one who played the evil twin? I used to watch soap operas with my neighbor, Mrs. Gable, years ago. She made the best iced tea, and she always put fresh mint from her garden in it. Mint will just take over your whole yard if you don't keep it contained in a pot. I tried growing cherry tomatoes in a pot on my patio last spring, but the squirrels took every single one of them before they even turned red. Squirrels are just so greedy, they take one bite and throw the rest on the ground...\n\nDoctor: Is there anything that could make you doubt your beliefs about your husband's family being involved in these events?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I feel like we're just... we're just saying the same things over and over. Doubt? No. Why would I doubt it? I'm here because for months now I’ve been trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. That's just the reality of it. When little billing errors show up at work, or someone at church suddenly acts funny toward me... no matter what records anyone pulls up, I know it ties back to them. There's no doubting what's right in front of you. \n\nJust like you can't doubt when the weather is about to turn. I can always feel it in my left knee, right behind the kneecap. It aches whenever a storm is coming in off the coast. We had a terrible storm last spring, knocked the power out for three days. All the food in my freezer went bad. I had just bought these frozen chicken pot pies that were on sale, buy one get one free. I love a good chicken pot pie, especially when the crust is really flaky. My aunt used to make the crust from scratch using shortening instead of butter. She said shortening makes it flakier. She kept it in this big blue tin canister on her counter. The canister had little white daisies painted on it. I tried painting daisies on a birdhouse once, but the yellow paint was too thin and it just ran down the sides like mustard. I hate mustard, actually. Even the smell of it makes my stomach turn. I went to a baseball game once and the guy next to me spilled mustard all over his jeans..."
